## Successor read stopped on optional Revision.service; fail-closed parser repair prepared — 2026-10-23 UTC

**Owner read result:** the pinned successor inspector stopped with `STOP: REVISION_METADATA_INVALID` before producing its receipt. No Cloud Run, traffic/scaling, schema, role, application-data, Floot or Production mutation was performed. The Cloud Shell default project `khedma-dl-e409d` is irrelevant because the inspector pins all Google targets explicitly to Preview V2.

The stop occurs at the first per-revision identity check: the service-scoped Cloud Run v2 `revisions.list` request returned at least one revision row whose optional/output-only `service` field did not equal the parser's required full service string. Google documents `Revision.service` as output-only, while the list parent and each full revision `name` already encode the exact parent service. ProtoJSON may omit default/optional output fields. Therefore requiring `service` unconditionally is stricter than the API contract and can reject a correctly scoped revision.

This repair does **not** trust an unscoped revision. It first validates each revision `name` against the exact `projects/${PROJECT}/locations/${REGION}/services/${SERVICE}/revisions/... ` scope, then allows `service` only when absent or exactly equal to the expected full service resource. Any foreign service value still stops. The redacted revision receipt adds only `service_field_present`; it never prints foreign resource values. One new regression raises the focused Python suite to **18 cases**.

Prepared inspector blob `c6038bb0c86c6b96a7875f3a8532c8a96182e884`, SHA256 `b0cf9085cfbed65c8bd0e0f55e0bd41d337c9ec986bc086391cbe744524aebbf`. The prior owner command must not be repeated. After this repair's exact-head CI/review and normal merge, run only the re-pinned command in NEXT-ACTION. No deploy, 036/037, writer-fence or Floot gate activation is authorized by this parser correction.

## Candidate image provenance closed; successor authority/lifecycle read prepared — 2026-10-23 UTC

**Owner strict status receipt:** at `2026-10-23T21:59:07.219961+00:00`, the preserved build `8b1b7aee-67f3-4721-ae76-f4bb60b4dc5e` reread as `SUCCESS` with `build_path_verified=true`. The exact source remains commit `ecd3ad8a16cd9820c599f16e75b1acf3e5a85966`, tree `3063508c52c1c348f8eeef59c49ce424608557ef`, source generation `1792786650146421`, manifest SHA256 `57431e14db8a68376629dfe61393e24785ec56e3d2f8dc98ccf052b82ba9ee2d` and archive SHA256 `38b921d2d297ebb8b57a8632e9f339251ac29fc76de32d76f6f236acf69016bf`.

The immutable backend image is now bound as `europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/khedmah-preview-v2-20261012/khedmah-preview/backend@sha256:2146d382d426568a510b6e76ff06129934e84beb86bc81c348454fe3e29e260e`; the Cloud Build worker image digest is `sha256:f772ab459889c67835bd959c2b9e9351f2c0752c4f989710ecda20c229f0733e`. The receipt was read-only: no upload/resubmission, Cloud Run, schema, role or application-data mutation occurred; `resubmission_allowed=false`. This closes the candidate build/image provenance gate only. `base_image_pinned=false` remains an explicit reproducibility limit, not a rejection of the observed immutable output digest.

The next unresolved boundary is **not** image construction. It is whether the intended deployer is policy-authorized for the exact Cloud Run service/image/runtime identity, what revisions/tags/scaling are currently reachable, and what database sessions exist before any maintenance design. Current Cloud Run documentation confirms that traffic migration leaves in-flight requests running and request timeout does not terminate container work; disabling service traffic also does not affect tagged revision URLs. Therefore traffic readback, scaling-to-zero, elapsed timeout or a single quiet database snapshot cannot by themselves prove writer quiescence.

This branch adds a read-only successor-gate inspector. It:
- GETs the exact Cloud Run v2 service and complete revision list and reports only bounded traffic/tag/scaling metadata;
- queries Policy Troubleshooter v3 for the deployer service account on seven exact permissions: Cloud Run service get/update, revision list, operation get, Artifact Registry download, runtime service-account actAs, and service usage; any denial, conditional/unknown state or API-unavailable result remains fail-closed;
- evaluates `run.operations.get` against a syntactically valid operation-resource probe, not the service resource; this is a preflight policy tuple only and does not assert a future operation id or conditional result will match;
- opens the existing migration secret only in process memory, connects through a private Cloud SQL Auth Proxy socket and runs one `BEGIN READ ONLY` `pg_stat_activity` count query; it returns no query text, client addresses, credentials or application rows;
- always reports `writer_quiescence_verified=false`, even when the runtime-session snapshot is zero. The snapshot is evidence for the future fencing design, never the fence itself.

Prepared inspector blob `1b2fbe8ac2e386e84cf69e1c8eb3c19d0fea577f`, SHA256 `acea3a09b68a9f751e893d9ade707d1338df66de4f96399143c11bfddf46b1e9`; **17 focused Python cases** plus a Node wrapper are added. The latest review repair also treats omitted `reconciling` as the ProtoJSON default `false` while retaining terminal/generation checks, and the restricted-stats fixture now matches the live SQL contract (`active`/idle detail null when unavailable). A direct recheck against the current Google REST contracts also pins Policy Troubleshooter full resource names to project ID (not project number) and accepts only the exact service-scoped full or short revision name forms. Codex review identified and this head fixes two fail-closed issues: the Unix-socket temporary path is shortened and tested against the actual 104-byte bound, and runtime sessions are counted by visible `usename` identity even when `pg_read_all_stats` is absent; activity-state details are then reported unavailable rather than fabricated as zero. No Cloud Run update, traffic/scaling change, SQL DDL/DML, IAM/role change, Floot publication or Production mutation is present.

**Blocking dependency audit repair on this same PR:** the exact-head Test & Verify run reached the blocking production audit only after the canonical root/backend/frontend test run passed. `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` then reported the newly published high-severity `body-parser` compressed-request denial-of-service advisory (GHSA-27r3-jxmh-p828) against resolved 2.3.0. The upstream fix is 2.4.0. Express 5.2.1 already permits `body-parser ^2.2.1`, so no direct manifest range change is required. The bounded manifest+lock repair changes one existing root security override (`qs 6.16.0 -> 6.16.1`) and exactly three of 324 package entries: `body-parser 2.3.0 -> 2.4.0`, its nested `content-type 2.0.0 -> 2.1.0`, and resolved `qs 6.16.0 -> 6.16.1`; the already installed `iconv-lite 0.7.3` remains unchanged. Express remains 5.2.1 with its existing `body-parser ^2.2.1` range. Registry URLs/integrities and dependency ranges match the upstream tagged package metadata, a dedicated Node regression test pins this production lock shape, and the existing install-policy test continues to require the resolved `qs` version to match the root override. No application behavior or Cloud resource is mutated by this dependency repair.

## Successful build default-worker metadata classified; strict digest read prepared — 2026-10-23 UTC

**Owner read-only classification receipt:** at `2026-10-23T21:41:00.784575+00:00`, the same saved attempt `87738bad-52b5-4a3f-b699-7dcf27652688` and build `8b1b7aee-67f3-4721-ae76-f4bb60b4dc5e` remain `SUCCESS`. The source generation is still `1792786650146421`; no build submission, upload, Cloud Run, schema, role, application-data or Production mutation occurred and `resubmission_allowed=false`.

The worker-execution ambiguity is now bounded: returned `options.pool` is present but exactly empty (zero keys), has no `name`, no WorkerPool resource and no unknown fields. Top-level `workerRelease` is present and exactly `legacy`. No nested `pool.workerRelease` or `pool.resolvedWorkerRelease` exists. The prior false nested-release booleans therefore mean **absent**, not contradictory values. Logging matches and omitted `substitutionOption` retains the request's fail-on-mismatch semantics.

Google's current REST schema defines `pool.name` as the concrete private WorkerPool selector, `workerRelease` as the build worker release/channel option, and `resolvedWorkerRelease` as output-only. The observed empty pool plus `legacy` top-level release is therefore materially narrower than a private-pool or nondefault-channel configuration. This receipt satisfies the previously documented narrow evidence gate for a validator-only repair.

This branch changes only strict response validation: it permits server materialization of **exactly** an empty `pool` and `workerRelease=legacy`, while retaining the existing omission/default handling for `substitutionOption`. Any nonempty pool, private-pool name, nested pool output metadata, non-`legacy` worker release, malformed/output-only resolved release or unknown option remains rejected. Four focused fail-closed cases raise the Python suite from 42 to **46 cases**. Prepared operator blob `28298b638543af0b718240fb40c10f66c3dce494`, SHA256 `4a129b8b3198a9aa8e1656195f1765afd376bcaa9e8554e1d5773a24cca7cf66`.

## Candidate build succeeded; worker execution semantics still require read-only classification — 2026-10-23 UTC

**Owner diagnostic receipt:** the existing attempt `87738bad-52b5-4a3f-b699-7dcf27652688`, build `8b1b7aee-67f3-4721-ae76-f4bb60b4dc5e`, was reread without mutation at `2026-10-23T21:03:04.446984+00:00`. Cloud Build reports `build_status=SUCCESS`; the exact source object generation `1792786650146421` is observed, no build was resubmitted, and `resubmission_allowed=false`. Strict acceptance remains false only because the returned `options` shape differs from the submitted request.

The bounded receipt reports option keys `logging`, `pool`, and `workerRelease`. Logging matches. The submitted `substitutionOption=MUST_MATCH` is absent from the returned JSON but the validator's documented-default comparison still matches. The two unresolved additions are `pool` and `workerRelease`; the prior diagnostic deliberately redacted their values. Therefore **do not** whitelist them yet.

Google's current Cloud Build REST schema documents `pool` as the optional WorkerPool specification and `pool.name` as the concrete private WorkerPool resource; it also documents top-level `workerRelease` as an execution option. The current release-channel documentation states that builds which do not specify a worker release use `legacy` by default until 2027-03-28. Those semantics mean the existing key-only receipt is insufficient to distinguish harmless server materialization of the default worker environment from a private-pool or nondefault worker configuration.

**Repository closure before this follow-up:** PR297 merged normally at `main=3ceafed0bf05ba438ca955d3fb92d4203ce6da6f`. Its main [Node.js CI 40022951776](https://github.com/khedma-sy/khedmah-digital-v1/actions/runs/40022951776) and [Test & Verify 40022951711](https://github.com/khedma-sy/khedmah-digital-v1/actions/runs/40022951711) both completed successfully; the canonical root/backend/frontend test run passed and blocking dependency audit reported zero vulnerabilities. The database transaction and Cloud Run rollout are separate operations. [Cloud Run traffic guidance](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/rollouts-rollbacks-traffic-migration) states that routing changes are not instantaneous and in-flight requests continue.

### Completed source repair

Fresh review found that the native document form's pending-state preflight did not protect a director from a concurrent approval/rejection: the backend intentionally retains a broader security override. A decided document returns 409 for every role. Omission preserves the separate legacy security override; bounded-role, self-review and latest-document checks remain intact.

Floot's server adapter now always supplies that literal precondition for native document approval, including the director's requests. The browser cannot select or remove it. A post-preflight 409 remains `not_applied`, with no automatic retry. The older serving controller does not enforce this new field, so the source change does not justify opening the gate against that artifact.

### Native Floot bounded approval forms and held execution

**Code validation and checkpoint:** Floot code version `1792604513416`, checkpoint `88feabe9-ed9b-4ebd-bd26-36cfed22b863` (**نماذج الموافقة البشرية مع بوابة تنفيذ مغلقة**), records **13 source/test files: five new and eight modified**. Exact readback matched all 13 files. The new files supply the immutable execution gate, dedicated approval transport and its spec, and the approval endpoint/schema; existing projection, read metadata, review UI/examples/styles and regression files carry the corresponding integration. Seven spec files passed with zero failures: `khedmahApprovalTransport`, `khedmahReviewContract`, `khedmahReviewTransport`, `khedmahReviewUi`, `khedmahPublicNavigation`, `khedmahAdmin` and `khedmahAdapterSecurity`; full TypeScript was clean. This is source/fixture validation, not a live decision receipt.

The forms bind explicit human confirmation to the displayed snapshot: Classifieds review/assessment revisions, product and profile microsecond revision strings, verification request/profile revision and mandatory trimmed notes, a driver document and its business locator, and Taxi driver/vehicle decimal revisions with required zone/reference/reason/expiry. The dedicated transport reconstructs only fixed approval routes and bodies, verifies permission through a fresh overview, rereads the selected queue, and requires the exact eligible item/revision before a possible dispatch. Driver latest eligibility uses all returned states and the unique greatest upload timestamp; ties at millisecond precision disable approval rather than guessing from IDs or array order. Rejection, suspension, owner-content/price editing, role changes and KORA decisions are outside this contract.

**Execution is held in code:** `Object.freeze({enabled:false})` is the server authority. The HTTP handler returns `503` with `outcome:"not_applied"` before any upstream request; no request field/header/query, environment switch or role can enable it. Queue metadata reports the same false gate, and the UI disables final submission if that metadata is false or missing. Forms remain reviewable. `enabled:true` occurs only in synthetic specs/examples, whose approval endpoint is intercepted; it does not enable the real handler.

The gate is required by a concrete runtime mismatch. Independent primary-source read of serving checkout `f4f8ce2b4b3d0efafedcddfe5f49e7fa4d783d9a`, [driver review service](https://github.com/khedma-sy/khedmah-digital-v1/blob/f4f8ce2b4b3d0efafedcddfe5f49e7fa4d783d9a/apps/backend/src/media/driver-document-review.service.ts), exact blob `037a37256a8a069b6c17bddadaf6a1267f22fe5e`, confirmed that its review transaction lacks main's latest-document, self-approval and pending-state guards. Fresh Floot reads cannot supply those atomic guarantees. The existing image provenance remains valid; a health version tag or a successful preflight does not make this old runtime compatible. Preserve the working director and do not add `smart_admin_approver` to that old role resolver.

The new write path preserves redacted HTTP outcomes: a preflight `404` is `not_applied`, while `404` after dispatch is `unknown` because a profile service can commit and then fail its result reread. Genuine `409` requires a fresh GET and renewed human review/confirmation. Timeout, abort or an invalid success receipt after dispatch remains uncertain, with no automatic retry. The UI locks before dispatch, ignores late results after close/unmount and quarantines the same snapshot across in-app refresh or edited notes. Only opaque SHA-256 fingerprints remain in memory; a full page reload resets that memory. These approval endpoints have no idempotency contract, and neither preflight nor the quarantine creates one. The unrelated generic write transport's 409-to-502 behavior remains unchanged.

A fresh `navigate_preview(page-examples)` again found no responding Floot window, so desktop/mobile visual acceptance remains unexecuted. The earlier rejected anonymous HTTP probes were not retried and establish no anonymous 401 or other HTTP receipt. The published baseline remains unchanged; no real approval, publication, role grant, backend deployment, cloud/schema change or persistent application-data write occurred.

### Confirmed platform contract gaps outside approval

These are confirmed source gaps retained from the platform-wide Floot audit, not merely missing populated-data acceptance. The compatibility, native review-read and held approval-form phases did not implement them.

| Journey | Confirmed remaining source gap |
| --- | --- |
| Customer order decisions | Customer tracking does not provide the customer's quoted-price confirmation or cancellation controls. Owner/merchant transitions do not complete that customer journey. |
| Retry and uncertain-response recovery | Checkout/creation attempt keys are regenerated on retry, and an uncertain response has no completed recovery/reconciliation flow before a fresh attempt. UUID format compatibility alone does not close duplicate-attempt risk. |
| Product publication media | Native product-image upload is absent from Floot even though an eligible product image is required before publication. Correct product/business IDs do not satisfy that prerequisite. |

These remain in the full Floot contract work; no repair or live acceptance for them is claimed by code checkpoints `83c0d313-2637-4a8b-86bb-8e11bb7f082c`, `172cabe3-aa78-4718-b651-494d14963cd7` or `88feabe9-ed9b-4ebd-bd26-36cfed22b863`.

## Production gate audit — open
Detailed gate requirements, completed observations and the next unresolved metadata-only read are in [PRODUCTION-RECONCILIATION.md](PRODUCTION-RECONCILIATION.md).

- Current role workflow has no backup input, backup freshness check or restore/PITR validation. Its runtime-containment recovery is not a tested backup restore.
- Canonical active-account and service-account-existence checks do not establish the current effective Secret Manager/IAM permissions.
- Direct secret IAM, returned project IAM, AliasManager/CloudAssetPolicyAnalyzer definitions and the selected execution's manifest comparison have been reviewed. Metadata for the two historical service accounts is the already-issued pending read; remaining custom-role/effective IAM, identity usage/retirement, aliases/version states, backup/PITR/restore evidence, actual PostgreSQL memberships and installed schema remain unverified. Historical project-level secret access is an unresolved reconciliation finding, not authorization to retire identities or bindings.

This source alignment does not prove full effective IAM or authorize any live binding change; the remaining cloud gates stay open.
- PREPARE may legitimately start without an `active` alias and inspect enabled numeric versions. Metadata does not prove any selector authenticates.
- `Rotate migration password before runtime cutover` creates a transition secret version and changes the migrator password before the later `prepare` SQL phase compares the actual system-role manifest. A syntactically valid hash is not prior proof of a match. Review the current manifest and recovery evidence before any dispatch.
- All database role workflow modes build an image; INVENTORY/VERIFY also deploy and execute Cloud Run jobs. Read existing execution/log/secret/backup metadata first. Do not dispatch a Production workflow merely because its SQL is read-only.

## Dependency security — bounded Multer update

- Published implementation: [`a7864998ae8d1e1a933b7a38c03910f7b8b3e048`](https://github.com/khedma-sy/khedmah-digital-v1/commit/a7864998ae8d1e1a933b7a38c03910f7b8b3e048), following MC-04. Only [package.json](../../package.json) and [package-lock.json](../../package-lock.json) change: existing Multer override `2.3.0` to `2.4.0`, its verified registry resolution/integrity, and removal of six unused dependencies from its removed `concat-stream` subtree. Nest remains `11.2.3`; no workflow, install-script or framework change.
- [Maintainer advisory GHSA-3pph-fpjx-jg34](https://github.com/expressjs/multer/security/advisories/GHSA-3pph-fpjx-jg34) describes aborted disk-storage uploads leaving orphaned files, affecting `>=2.2.0,<2.4.0` and patched in `2.4.0`. The two moderate audit entries represented Multer and its transitive effect on the Nest adapter. They pre-existed MC-01: #249 Test job `111637373300` and main `b7b6d0aa...` Test job `111643952230` already reported them.
- Fresh local native npm `11.9.0` installation with scripts disabled resolved Multer `2.4.0` from Nest's own context. The subsequent audit reported **zero known vulnerabilities** in its audited dependency scope, compared with two moderate baseline entries; this is a dated dependency scan, not a complete security certification.
- Backend build, **30 HTTP/security cases and three existing install-policy tests** passed. Package blobs: manifest `1ca0ed19772a9b108c4492d804bdb36c37824c2c`; lock `29ad425aead5b9d89f1d8bcc438a982bdf14b348`. Independent security review reported no blockers; the grouped #252 head subsequently passed its own CI and Preview and entered main, as recorded above.
- Installer limit: a temporary compatible npm `10.9.3` invocation generated the accepted lock. npm `11.9.0` lock regeneration ignored the root override, and its `ls` diagnostic flags the upstream exact `2.2.0` declaration for both baseline `2.3.0` and repaired `2.4.0`. Those regeneration results were discarded; fresh native installation, runtime resolution, audit, build and tests verified the accepted lock. No project toolchain pin or vendor metadata was changed.
- Source searches found no application Multer multipart/interceptor/disk-storage wiring; existing media uploads use JSON/base64. Deployed exploitability was not established, and no Production rollout occurred.

## Automatic workflow boundary for this follow-up

At reviewed main `b7b6d0aa...`, with the same workflow definitions on `dba1b5ee...`, the decoder/dependency/capture changes trigger ordinary main CI and guarded PR Preview cleanup on merge, with no configured automatic Production deployment. The root package change also triggers Google readiness's repository checks and unconfigured Android debug build on the PR; its Production secret job requires `workflow_dispatch`. PR Preview does build/deploy isolated nonproduction resources. No workflow definition changes in this follow-up. This source audit does not certify effective environment/IAM settings or out-of-repository triggers, and closes no Production gate.

## Floot transport finding — conditional, not implemented

[Candidate B evidence](../floot-migration/AUTH-API-CONTRACT.md#candidate-b-compatibility-evidence) identifies a same-site HTTPS frontend/API origin pair as viable in principle. Cross-site `floot.app` authentication remains subject to third-party-cookie restrictions; exact CORS/CSRF, media URL resolution, logout and Firebase behavior need real Safari/Chromium proof on an approved nonproduction arrangement. The existing Business Desk source uses its own authentication; it is not a completed Khedmah integration. Target project, plan and domain arrangement remain open; no Floot resource or DNS was changed.
